_The system denied 1 write attempt: this role writes nothing; reproduce by reading the code, the task documents and the existing tests, or by running the declared checks. A denial here is not a syntax problem._

### Comparisons

I ran `scripts/design/compare.sh` for Variedades, VariedadesCompu and VariedadDetalle. All three ran fine. I also checked the components UI Designer names against `pantallas/`, `Componentes.dc.html` and `componentes.ts`. The prototype has nothing drawn for removal, Archivadas or the archived page, so I judged those new screens on the text, against the components they say they are built from.

**Variedades (phone) and VariedadesCompu**
- **Fidelity:** the same screens, with archived varieties left out of the list and the summary line.
- **"Archivadas (N)" at the foot:** it reuses the semibold text treatment of "Todas las entradas" (16px phone, 15px computer) and is absent when N = 0. It is not drawn but can be composed directly.
- **States:** the toasts, the gone notice and computer reselection are all specified.

**VariedadDetalle (live)**
- **The comparison answers UI Designer's open question.** The built `/varieties/{low}` already uses "Editar" links for Aviso de stock bajo and En el catálogo, with a read-only attribute summary. The inline inputs in the prototype are the agreed departure, so the archived page starts from the built page.
- **States:** both removal states (no history and with history), stale, gone and loading are present.
- **The cited style is wrong.** UI Designer says the removal action uses the destructive style of "Borrar este precio". That control is a neutral trash icon in the prototype and a "Borrar" text in the built app. The real destructive models are:
  - the Boton "destructivo" variant in Componentes;
  - the "Eliminar" text link in ConfiguracionCategorias.
- **"Archivar variedad"** should use the "suave" Boton, so it reads calmer than "Eliminar variedad".
- **Placement:** the built page has no footnote, so the action goes after "En el catálogo".

**Confirmation dialogs**
- Both reuse the Estados "Antes de borrar" frame.
- The grams line and the zero-stock variant are present.

  - "Cobrar" is a dark primary pill, not a secondary button. "Restaurar" on every row should use the "suave" Boton instead.
  - There is no drawn "Oculta" chip to copy. "Archivada" should be an Insignia with tono "neutro": a word, with no MarcaEstado shape.

**Archived variety's page (new)**
- It is the built page, read-only. "Archivada" is shown in words, and "Oculta" too when the variety is hidden.
- Every control the PRD names is removed.
- **Rename link:** the "Editar" link next to the name should be listed explicitly as removed.

**Estados**

**approved**: every screen and state the PRD requires is present and can be built from the product's own components. The findings are component-naming slips and placement details, all observations.

## PM: consolidation

### Verdict

**approved**: Both validations approved. Every job can be completed, every failure has a way back, and no finding stops a job. Every finding is friction, so all of them go to the PR as observations.

### PRD

#### Problem

Today a variety can only be hidden from the catalog. A hidden variety still shows in Variedades (marked "Oculta"), in Nueva venta and in every other list. So a variety loaded by mistake, or one the business no longer works with, can never leave the lists. Sellers hide it and live with it, rename it to something like "NO USAR", or leave it sold out. The owner asked (2026-10-06): "¿No se pueden borrar variedades?"

#### Job story

When a variety I loaded by mistake, or one I no longer work with, keeps showing in my lists and pickers, I want to take it out from my phone in a few taps, deleting it if it never moved and archiving it if it has history, so my lists show only what I still sell, no past sale, stock, cost or profit figure changes, and I can bring it back exactly as it was if I start selling it again.

This applies the varieties and navigation job to the end of a variety's life. It is not a new job.

#### Flows

**Delete a variety that never moved**
- **Starts at:** Variedades, a tab root (a list on the phone, two panes on a computer).
- **Steps:**
  1. Open the variety's page ("‹ Variedades"). On a computer the first unarchived variety is already selected.
  2. Tap "Eliminar variedad". With no history, it is the only removal action shown.
  3. Confirm in the destructive ConfirmDialog.
  4. Back on Variedades: the toast "Variedad eliminada" shows and the row is gone. On a computer the next variety by name is selected, else the previous one, else the empty state.
- **Recovery:**
  - Cancel changes nothing.
  - If history appeared after the page loaded, the delete is refused, and the page refreshes to show "Archivar variedad".
  - If a partner already deleted it, "Eso ya no está. La lista ya está al día." shows on the list, or "Eso ya no está." with "Volver a Variedades" on a detail.
  - The name is free at once.
- **Ends at:** Variedades, with the variety, its prices, its threshold and its attributes gone.
- **Touches:** 3 on the phone from the Variedades tab (4 from another tab). On a computer, 2 to 3.

**Archive a variety that has history**
- **Steps:**
  1. Open the variety's page.
  2. Tap "Archivar variedad". With history, it is the only removal action shown.
  3. Confirm. When stock is not zero, the confirmation names the grams on hand. With zero stock it only asks.
  4. Back on Variedades (a decision of the flow): the toast "Variedad archivada" shows. The variety leaves the list and the summary line, the selection moves as in delete, and "Archivadas (N)" appears or counts up.
- **Recovery:** Cancel, a variety already gone and a stale page all work as in delete.
- **Ends at:** Variedades. The variety is gone from Lo que te queda, every picker, the catalog text and the pinned catalog, and the pin is updated. Its hidden flag is kept. Past records keep its plain name.
- **Touches:** the same as delete.

**Find archived varieties and restore one**
- **By the card:**
  1. Tap "Archivadas (N)" at the foot of Variedades. It is absent when N = 0.
  2. The card `/varieties/archived` opens, with "‹ Variedades".
  3. Tap "Restaurar". There is no confirmation. The toast "Variedad restaurada" shows, and the variety returns everywhere it qualifies.
- **By the page:** from the card, open the archived page (read-only, "‹ Archivadas") and tap "Restaurar".
- **Recovery:**
  - A variety already gone or already restored gives "Eso ya no está. La lista ya está al día." and the list refreshes.
  - To delete an archived variety that has lost all its history, the seller restores it, then deletes it.
  - Adding or renaming a variety to an archived name is refused inline, pointing to Archivadas.
- **Touches:** 2 from Variedades (3 from another tab). 3 by way of the page.

**A form left open while a partner archives the variety**
- In Nueva venta, the stock entry form, the price form or an edit form, saving is refused with "Eso ya no está", keeping what was typed where the screen allows.
- In Nueva venta the line shows the generic gone message and the form stays open.
- The seller picks another variety, closes with "Cerrar", or restores the variety and starts again. This costs 1 to 2 extra touches.

**Existing records of an archived variety**
- Ventas, a sale's detail, the customer's account, Todas las entradas, Análisis, Ganancia and Caja, and the export are unchanged. They show the plain name, with no tag.
- Entries stay editable and deletable. A sale containing the variety can be deleted, Deshacer included, and Asignar cliente works.
- An entry edit cannot move onto another archived variety, because the picker leaves archived varieties out.

**Unchanged jobs:** Nueva venta, "+ Entrada de stock", the price entry, "Mandar catálogo" and Inicio keep the same steps and taps. Their lists and pickers only have fewer rows.

#### Success criteria

1. **Out of the way in a few taps.** From a variety's own page, on phone and computer, the seller takes it out of every list and picker. The app offers Delete only when the variety has no history, and Archive otherwise, and never a delete that will be refused.
2. **The books don't move.** After archiving, past sales and their lines, stock, cost of goods, stock shortfall, Ganancia, Caja, every Análisis figure and customer balances read exactly as before, to the peso and the hundredth of a gram.
3. **Comes back whole.** Restoring brings back the variety's prices, stock, entries, threshold, catalog attributes and hidden flag, in every list and picker it qualifies for.
4. **The domain decides.** The domain itself refuses a delete with history.
5. **Archived varieties stay findable.** The seller can always see what they archived and restore it.
6. **The rules that already hold still hold.** Businesses stay isolated (ADR-0004). States are told by word and shape, never by colour alone, in both themes. Toasts carry no data.

#### Behaviour

##### Two actions, chosen by history
- **History** is any sale line of the variety (gifts and special prices included) or any stock entry of it (any origin, an adjustment of either sign included). It is worked out live and never stored.
- **Not history:** price entries, the threshold and catalog attributes. They go with a deleted variety.
- **Delete** is offered only with no history; the variety is gone for good. **Archive** is offered with history.
- Delete is offered again once the last sale or entry is deleted.
- A delete with history is refused by the domain with its own code in `contract.json` (ADR-0008), repeating no name or amount (ADR-0005).
- The check and the delete are one guarded write: nothing is ever orphaned.
- A variety already gone answers 404: "Eso ya no está. La lista ya está al día."

##### Archiving
- It is allowed with stock above, at or below zero, always after a confirmation.
- When stock is not zero, the confirmation names the grams on hand, for example "Te quedan 12 g. Si la archivás, dejan de verse en Inicio y en las listas, pero el stock queda guardado." The exact wording is design's.
- Archiving and restoring never create an entry and never change stock, lots, cost of goods, pending cost, prices or any figure.
- **An archived variety leaves:**
  - Variedades' list and its summary line;
  - Lo que te queda;
  - the pickers of Nueva venta, the stock entry form and the price entry form;
  - the catalog text and its "what does not go out" list;
  - the pinned catalog.
- It does not count for "Arrancá por las variedades".
- Archiving and restoring are upkeep triggers (pinned-catalog.md).
- Archive and hide are independent: the hidden flag is kept and comes back as it was.

##### Names
- An archived variety keeps its name reserved (case and spacing ignored).
- Adding or renaming a variety to that name is refused inline with its own fixed refusal, which points to Archivadas and repeats nothing typed.
- An archived variety is renamed only after it is restored.
- A deleted variety's name is free at once.

##### Past and existing records
- Past records show the plain name, with no tag and no figure changed: Ventas and a sale's detail, the customer's account, Todas las entradas, Análisis, Results and the export.
- Existing records stay workable (ADR-0009): an entry can be edited or deleted, a sale can be deleted (Deshacer included), and Asignar cliente still works.
- An entry edit keeps its own archived variety, and can never move onto another archived one.
- Nothing new is recorded against an archived variety: no sale line, stock entry or price entry. A reference to one answers as gone.
- A form left open is refused on save under accounts.md's "Eso ya no está" rule.

##### Isolation
- Every new route joins the isolation sweep and the gated-route test.
- Another business's variety, archived or not, answers 404.

#### Screens

**Variedades (phone) and VariedadesCompu (tab root)**
- These are the existing screens.
- Archived varieties are left out of the list and the summary line.
- "Archivadas (N)" sits at the foot, beside "Todas las entradas", with the same text-link treatment. It is absent when N = 0.
- The toasts "Variedad eliminada" and "Variedad archivada" use the existing `Estados` toast.
- A stale or gone removal gives the "Eso ya no está. La lista ya está al día." notice.
- **Computer:**
  - on opening, the first unarchived variety by name is selected;
  - after archiving or deleting it, the next by name is selected, else the previous one, else the empty pane, which shows no figure.
- **Shared states:** loading, retry and "Sin conexión".

**VariedadDetalle (live, "‹ Variedades")**
- The structure is unchanged: the summary line, "+ Entrada" and "Nuevo precio", and the sections Stock y entradas, Precios, Aviso de stock bajo and En el catálogo (with "Editar" links, as built).
- One removal action sits at the end of the page, set apart from the per-price delete. It reads "Eliminar variedad" with no history, or "Archivar variedad" with history, never both.
- On a computer it sits at the foot of the right pane.
- **States:**
  - no history or with history;
  - stale: the delete is refused, and the page refreshes in place to show "Archivar variedad";
  - gone: "Eso ya no está." with "Volver a Variedades";
  - loading, error and offline.

**Confirmation dialogs (the Estados "Antes de borrar" frame)**
- **Delete:** asks to delete the variety for good, and says its prices, threshold and catalog attributes go with it. The pair is "No, dejala / Sí, borrala", with destructive emphasis.
- **Archive:** the same frame, with no destructive emphasis. It shows the grams line when stock is not zero, and a plain question at zero. The wording is design's.
- **States:** open; working (buttons disabled); a refusal closes it and refreshes the page; a variety already gone closes it with the gone notice.

**Archivadas card (`/varieties/archived`, "‹ Variedades", no "+ Nueva venta")**
- It is built from the Variedades list rows.
- **Each row:** the name, the word "Archivada" where the status word sits (with no live status mark), and "Restaurar". The rest of the row opens the archived page.
- **States:**
  - filled;
  - empty, by a direct link or after the last restore: a fixed line such as "No tenés variedades archivadas";
  - after a restore: the toast "Variedad restaurada", the row leaves the list and the count updates;
  - already gone: the gone notice;
  - loading, error and offline.
- **Computer:** two panes, with the first archived variety selected. "Restaurar" is on the row and on the right pane.

**Archived variety's page (`/varieties/{low}`, read-only, "‹ Archivadas")**
- It is VariedadDetalle read-only.
- **Shows:** "Archivada" in words in the summary line, and "Oculta" when hidden. The sections are plain read-only text.
- **Removes:**
  - "+ Entrada" and "Nuevo precio";
  - the per-price delete;
  - the threshold switch and every "Editar";
  - rename;
  - the removal action.
- **Single action:** "Restaurar". It is one tap with no confirmation, shows "Variedad restaurada", and returns to Archivadas.
- **States:**
  - gone: "Eso ya no está." with "Volver a Variedades";
  - restored by a partner: the page opens as live;
  - loading, error and offline.
- **"Ver todas" lists:** they open as read lists. Entries keep their edit and delete, and there is no per-price delete.

**VariedadNueva and the rename form**
- These are the existing forms.
- A new inline refusal uses the existing field-error treatment, for example "Ese nombre es de una variedad archivada. Fijate en Archivadas." It has no link, repeats nothing typed and keeps the typed text.

**Nueva venta, EntradaStock and PrecioNuevo**
- The pickers leave archived varieties out.
- **A form left open while a partner archives:**
  - Nueva venta: the line shows the generic gone message and the form stays open;
  - EntradaStock and PrecioNuevo: the form keeps what was typed and shows "Eso ya no está. La lista ya está al día.".
- An entry edit keeps its own archived variety, shown as a plain name.

**Catalogo, Inicio (InicioOscuro, InicioCompu) and the past-record screens**
- There is no visual change.
- Archived varieties are absent from the preview and "what does not go out" (no reason chip), from Lo que te queda and from "Arrancá por las variedades".
- Ventas, VentaDetalle, ClienteFicha, Todas las entradas and Análisis show the plain name, with unchanged figures.

**Estados**
- Three new fixed toasts: "Variedad eliminada", "Variedad archivada" and "Variedad restaurada". They carry no data and no "Deshacer".
- Both themes come from tokens.
- "Archivada" is a word in a chip, and the removal action is a labelled button: no state is told by colour alone.

#### Metrics

- **Offered action (criteria 1, 4)**, a matrix, 100% passing:
  - Delete is offered exactly when there is no history, and Archive otherwise;
  - history is tested from each source alone: a list line, a gift, a special price, an entry of each origin, and both adjustment signs;
  - prices, a threshold and attributes alone leave Delete offered;
  - deleting the last sale or entry offers Delete again;
  - the domain refuses a delete with history with its code, through the API.
- **No orphan (criterion 4):** a test over real SQLite races a delete against a sale line and against an entry. 0 rows reference a missing variety.
- **Books unchanged (criterion 2):** `GET /analysis` (every window), `GET /panel`'s period figures, sale details, balances and accounts, and stock, cost of goods and pending grams per variety are captured before archiving, after archiving and after restoring. 0 differences.
- **Out of every list and picker (criterion 1):** the archived variety is absent from the list, the summary line, Lo que te queda, the three pickers, the catalog text, its "does not go out" list and the pin. Upkeep edits the pin on archive and on restore exactly when the text changes. 100% passing.
- **Comes back whole (criterion 3):** after a restore, prices, stock, entries, threshold, attributes and the hidden flag (tested both hidden and shown) are equal to before. 100% passing.
- **Names:** an archived variety's name is refused on add and on rename with its own code. A deleted variety's name is accepted at once.
- **Nothing new:** a new line, entry or price against an archived variety is refused as gone. An entry edit keeps its own archived variety and cannot move onto another. Entry edit and delete, sale delete (Deshacer included) and Asignar cliente still work.
- **Findable (criterion 5):** "Archivadas (N)" shows the right count and is absent at 0. Restore takes 1 tap from the card or the page. The archived page offers only "Restaurar".
- **Taps (criterion 1):** archiving or deleting takes the action plus its confirmation on the phone, and a computer takes no more.
- **Isolation and no echo (criterion 6):** every new route is in the sweep and the gated-route test, and answers 404 from another business. No refusal or toast carries a name or an amount.
- **No curl:** the end-to-end run deletes a fresh variety, archives one with a sale, finds it in Archivadas, opens its read-only page and restores it.
- **Design (criterion 6):** `scripts/design/compare.sh` runs before and after for the list, the page, Archivadas and the archived page, on phone and computer, in both themes, attached to the PR.

#### Product documents to change

- `varieties.md`: both actions, history, names, Archivadas, the archived page, the routes and the metrics. The "Deleting a variety" out-of-scope line is reversed.
- `CONTEXT.md`: **Archived variety** and **Variety history** are new. **Hidden variety**, **Variety** and **Catalog** are revised.
- `catalog.md`, `pinned-catalog.md`, `results.md` and `navigation.md`.
- `scripts/design/screens.json` and `pantallas/`: add the Archivadas card and the archived page.

#### Assumptions

- The export holds the archived flag as a stored fact, and the import treats an archived variety like any other record.
- The first-run checklist is unaffected in practice.

#### Out of scope

- Merging varieties, or taking over an archived variety's name.
- Deleting, archiving or restoring several varieties at once.
- Archiving or deleting from the list itself.
- An "archivada" tag on past records.
- Deleting a variety with history, by any path.
- Moving a stock entry onto an archived variety.
- Live updates between partners.

### Questions for the human

None.

### Observations on the design

- **All new screens:** nothing is drawn yet in `pantallas/` for the Archivadas card, the archived page, the removal action, the two dialogs, "Archivadas (N)", the reserved-name refusal or the form-left-open state. They are built from existing components.
- **VariedadDetalle, removal action:** place it so it cannot be confused with the per-price "Borrar este precio".
- **VariedadDetalle, removal action style:** the design cites the style of "Borrar este precio", which is not destructive. Use Componentes' Boton "destructivo" or the ConfiguracionCategorias "Eliminar" link for delete, and the "suave" Boton for "Archivar variedad".
- **VariedadDetalle, placement:** the built page has no footnote, so the action goes right after "En el catálogo".
- **Delete confirmation:** say whether the dialog may name the variety.
- **Archive confirmation:** the button pair is under-specified. A suggestion: "No, dejala / Sí, archivala".
- **Archive flow:** landing on Variedades after archiving should be recorded as a decision.
- **Archivadas row:** "Restaurar" should be the "suave" Boton, not modelled on "Cobrar". "Archivada" should be an Insignia with tono "neutro".
- **Archivadas (computer):** say where "Restaurar" sits across the panes, and where the right pane goes after a restore.
- **Archived page, controls:**
  - list the rename "Editar" link and the attribute controls explicitly as removed;
  - put "Restaurar" in place of "+ Entrada / Nuevo precio", as a full-width suave button;
  - say where the person lands after restoring.
- **Archived page, "Ver todas":** VerTodas needs a state without per-price delete.
- **Catalog attributes:** the prototype draws inline inputs, while the built page and `varieties.md` use "Editar" forms (the agreed departure). The prototype stays out of step with them.
- **Reserved-name refusal:** there is no link to Archivadas, so the seller leaves the form by hand. A link would need the unsaved-changes confirmation.

## Interview: Tech Lead efímero→Tech Lead

### Round 1: questions

### Round 1: where it lands, who filters, how it splits

Failure I can already name: `SqlVarieties.name_taken` and `all()` (app/varieties/adapters/sql_varieties.py) count every row of the business with no notion of archived. If the plan only adds a flag column and filters in the router, `GET /varieties`, `name_taken` and every other reader of `VarietyRow` (sales `sql_sales.py`, stock `sql_draws.py`, results `assemble.py`, catalog) each decide alone whether an archived variety exists. Some must hide it (pickers, catalog, stock rows) and some must not (period figures, sale views, accounts, export). Per-reader decisions drift.

❓ **Q1** - **Where "has history" lives**: Deleting needs to know if a variety has any sale line or stock entry, and `varieties` cannot import `sales` or `stock` (ADR-0006). What port does `varieties` declare, who implements it, and where is it wired in `app/main.py`? A read-then-delete races with a concurrent sale or entry (ADR-0012/0019).
➡️ Recommendation: the port is declared by `varieties` (`has_history(variety_id)`), implemented by an adapter that queries the sales and stock tables, and wired in `main.py`. The delete is one guarded statement (`DELETE ... WHERE id=? AND business_id=? AND NOT EXISTS (sale_line) AND NOT EXISTS (stock_entry)`), with a rowcount of 0 mapped to the refusal or a 404. Also say what stops a sale or entry being inserted against the variety between the check and the commit.

❓ **Q2** - **The stored flag and its migration**: §11 requires `is_` for booleans, and `tests/migrations/test_naming.py` checks it. Is the column `is_archived` or `archived_at`? Is the migration a plain `ADD COLUMN` with a server default, or a rebuild as in ADR-0021? Does `name_key` uniqueness stay as is, so an archived variety keeps its name (the PRD refusal)?
➡️ Recommendation: `archived_at` (nullable timestamp), a plain `ADD COLUMN` with no rebuild, and the unique `(business_id, name_key)` constraint untouched. It needs no default backfill and keeps the archiving moment for free. Add a downgrade test. Confirm ADR-0015 classifies the column as exported by convention, and say how.

❓ **Q3** - **Filter fan-out and its proof**: The default list, catalog qualifying set and "does not go out" list, results stock rows and "Arrancá por las variedades", and the three pickers must hide archived varieties. Period figures (ADR-0020), sale views, accounts, Todas las entradas, Análisis rows and the export must not. How do you make "hide" and "show" explicit, rather than leaving a `WHERE archived_at IS NULL` in each query?
➡️ Recommendation: no implicit default. Each reader port passes `include_archived` explicitly, or exposes two named methods (`usable()` and `all_including_archived()`). Add a test that archives a variety with history and asserts the books (period figures, sale views, accounts, export) are identical before and after. That is the proof of criterion 2. Where is that test placed, and is it one test or one per reader?

❓ **Q4** - **A stale write against an archived variety**: Another tab holds a picker showing the variety, then it is archived. A sale, entry or price entry for it must answer the `gone` code, not 404 and not success. Which module decides, given `sales` and `stock` cannot import `varieties`? Does the guard go inside the insert (atomic) or in a prior read?
➡️ Recommendation: each caller (sales, stock, varieties prices) already has a port to look up the variety. Extend each existing lookup to return a state (`usable`, `archived`, `missing`) and keep a single check inside the same transaction. Register the three refusals in `web/src/api/contract.json` with kinds per ADR-0008. Also add `/varieties/archived` to `SHARED_FRONT_PATHS` and `sharedFrontRoutes` (ADR-0024/0030).

❓ **Q5** - **Subtask split and size**: Which piece carries more than about four production modules plus tests plus an ADR and needs its own seam? My candidate split is: (a) flag, migration, domain, repository and `has_history` port, with the ADR; (b) filtering in catalog, results and stock plus the books-unchanged test; (c) pickers and stale-write `gone` in sales, stock and prices; (d) routes, refusals, `contract.json` and shared paths; (e) front screens; (f) pinned-catalog upkeep triggers on archive and restore (ADR-0014, after commit under the lock). (b) and (c) touch the most hexagons.
➡️ Recommendation: do (a) first and alone, since all others depend on the port and flag. Then (b), (c), (d) and (f) can run in parallel against a stubbed port, and (e) last. If (b) crosses catalog, results and stock it is too big, so split it per hexagon. State which seam each piece tests through (an in-memory fake `Varieties`, or SQLite with the real adapter).

### Invariants proposed
- Every query that reads varieties or their referencing records states explicitly whether archived varieties are included, with no implicit default filter.
- No period figure, sale view, account or export query filters on the archived flag.
- Deleting a variety is a single guarded statement that checks the absence of history in the same statement, never a read followed by a delete.
- The new archive column name and its migration follow §11 and pass `tests/migrations/test_naming.py`, with a tested downgrade.

### Round 1: answers

### Where this lands (read before answering)

- `variety` is owned by `app/varieties/` (`VarietyRow`, `SqlVarieties`). Other hexagons reach it **adapter to adapter** by building `SqlVarieties` themselves: `sales/adapters/price_book.py`, `stock/adapters/variety_catalog.py`, `stock/adapters/stock_figures.py` and `catalog/adapters/sql_catalog_varieties.py`. `sql_sales.py` and `sql_draws.py` also join `VarietyRow` for names. Five tables hold a foreign key to `variety.id`: `price_entry`, `sale_line`, `stock_entry`, `stock_threshold` and `catalog_attributes`. `app/db.py` turns on `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON` for both drivers.
- Period figures already leave varieties out: `SqlStockFigures.read` builds draws from `for_varieties(None)`, and only the **stock rows** loop over `SqlVarieties.all()`. "Arrancá por las variedades" is decided in the browser from `GET /varieties` (`Inicio.tsx`), so it follows that route's default with no backend change.
- The varieties router is already mounted with `upkeep_gated` (`app/main.py`). Every write on it, archive, restore and delete included, schedules upkeep after the commit (ADR-0014) with no new wiring.

**Q1**: Accepted, with three changes: who implements the check, the dependents, and the race after the delete.
- **The check lives in varieties' own repository.** It does not go behind a port implemented elsewhere: the guard has to sit in the same statement as the delete, and only the owner of `variety` can write that statement. `SqlVarieties` (adapters) imports `SaleLineRow` and `StockEntryRow`. Adapters may cross hexagons, as `sql_sales.py` already does with `VarietyRow`; domain and application stay clean.
  - One private predicate, `_history_exists(variety_id)`, is two `EXISTS` subqueries filtered on `variety_id` and on `business_id`.
  - Two port methods on `Varieties` share that predicate:
    - `has_history(variety_id) -> bool`, for the page's offered action;
    - `remove_unused(variety_id) -> bool`, which runs `DELETE FROM variety WHERE id=:id AND business_id=:b AND NOT (<history>) RETURNING id`.
  - This is the same lookup/spend pairing as ADR-0019.
- **Dependents go first, inside the same transaction.** With foreign keys on, `price_entry`, `stock_threshold` and `catalog_attributes` must go before `variety`. Each owner deletes its own rows (ADR-0018's rule):
  - `price_entry` is varieties' own;
  - for the other two, varieties declares `VarietyLeaving(Protocol): def leaving(self, variety_id: int) -> None` in `application/ports.py`. It is implemented by a stock adapter (threshold) and a catalog adapter (attributes), and composed in `app/main.py` as a `(Session, business_id)` factory on `app.state`. This is ADR-0006's own "composite" consequence, not a second mechanism.
- **The use case, `delete_variety`, runs in this order:**
  1. `get`. Missing → 404 `variety.not_found`. Archived → 404 `variety.archived` (restore first; there is no second path).
  2. `leaving.leaving(id)`, then the price entries, then `remove_unused`.
  3. If no row comes back: `transaction.rollback()` and the refusal `variety.has_history` (409, kind `refusal`).
  4. Otherwise one commit.

  The writes are one transaction, and its first DELETE takes SQLite's write lock. The guard is still in the deciding statement itself, so correctness does not rest on lock timing.
- **What stops an insert between check and commit: the foreign key.** SQLite serialises writers.
  - A sale or entry that commits first makes the guarded DELETE return nothing, and the delete is refused.
  - A delete that commits first makes the later `sale_line` or `stock_entry` insert fail with a foreign-key `IntegrityError`.
  - Sales' and stock's repositories (and varieties' own price-entry and catalog's attributes writes) translate that error, **outside the `except` block** (ADR-0005), into their existing gone code: `sale.variety_not_found` and `variety.not_found`. Today that race would be a 500.
  - The race test in the metrics drives both orders over a real SQLite file with two connections.

**Q2**: Accepted.
- **Column:** `variety.archived_at`, `UtcDateTime`, nullable. It passes §11 (`_at` on a datetime column). Restore sets it back to `NULL`. In the domain, `Variety` gains `archived_at: datetime | None` and an `is_archived` property; no boolean column is added.
- **Migration:** `0012`, revising `0011`, a plain `op.add_column` with no server default and no backfill: every existing variety is unarchived. It is not a rebuild, and Turso and libSQL take `ADD COLUMN` as is.
  - The downgrade is `batch_alter_table(...).drop_column`, since SQLite's drop needs the batch path. It refuses loudly if any variety is archived, so a downgrade never silently brings back a variety the seller took out.
  - A migration test runs upgrade then downgrade over a seeded business with both drivers, as ADR-0021's test does.
- **The name constraint stays as it is.** `uq_variety_business_id_name_key` stays untouched. An archived variety keeps its name because its row keeps its `name_key`.
- **The port distinguishes who holds a name.** `name_taken` becomes `name_holder(name, *, other_than) -> NameHolder | None` (`LIVE`, `ARCHIVED`), so `register_variety` and `rename_variety` raise `NameTaken` (`variety.name_taken`) or the new `NameHeldByArchived` (`variety.name_archived`, 409, kind `refusal`, fixed text, nothing interpolated).
- **ADR-0015 needs no change to the command.** The column's segments `archived` and `at` hit nothing in the secret set. The column reflects as a datetime, not `OTHER`, so it is exported as an ISO instant with `Z`. `variety` already has `name` for references. I will add the column to the existing migrated-schema classification tests' expectations, so the proof is a test, not this sentence.

**Q3**: Accepted in spirit; I overrule the mechanism. There is no `include_archived` flag.
- **Two named methods replace `all()`.** `Varieties.all()` is removed, and `listed()` (unarchived) and `archived()` replace it. `get(id)` answers any variety with its state, and each caller reads `is_archived`. Removing `all()` makes every current caller a type error under `mypy --strict` until it chooses. A boolean parameter with a default is exactly the silent default the invariant forbids, and one without a default reads worse than two names.
- **Today's four `all()` callers, decided:**

  | Caller | Uses |
  |---|---|
  | `list_varieties`, behind `GET /varieties` | `listed()`; `GET /varieties?archived=true` → `archived()` |
  | `SqlCatalogVarieties.on` (qualifying set and "does not go out") | `listed()` |
  | `SqlStockFigures.read`'s stock rows (Lo que te queda) | `listed()` |
  | `SqlVarietyCatalog.ids` (`GET /stock`) | `listed()`; `GET /stock?archived=true` feeds the archived page's read-only summary |

- **The import's eligibility counts archived varieties.** Its `has_records` stays on an explicit count over the whole table, so an archived variety counts as a record.
- **Readers that join `VarietyRow` only for a name do not change.** `sql_sales.py` (`list_views`, sale views) and `sql_draws.py` (period figures, ADR-0020's scan) gain no filter. A test pins that they do not need one.
- **Archived varieties use a query parameter, not `/varieties/archived`, as the API path.** That keeps the front's `/varieties/archived` an exact shared path whose API side does not exist, so a browser navigation and a JSON call cannot meet on one path.
- **The proof is one behaviour test.** It is written first, at the HTTP seam, in `tests/varieties/test_archive_moves_no_figure.py`. It covers every reader together, because criterion 2 is a single claim about the whole set of books.
  - It seeds a history with:
    - two varieties, one to archive;
    - sales left owing and paid;
    - a later payment;
    - a purchase, a gift and a special price;
    - a negative adjustment with costed grams and one with pending grams;
    - an expense.
  - It captures `GET /analysis` for every month and the all-time window, `GET /panel`'s periods, every `GET /sales/{id}`, every customer's balance and account, and the archived variety's stock and pending grams through `GET /stock?archived=true` and the sale lines' costs.
  - It captures that set before archiving, after archiving and after restoring, and asserts equality.
- **One small test per hiding reader:** absence from `GET /varieties`, `GET /catalog` (text and "does not go out"), `GET /panel` stock rows and `GET /stock`, under each owner's `tests/<capability>/`.

**Q4**: Accepted, with two changes.
- **The check is a prior read through each caller's existing lookup, not inside the insert.** The race it leaves is harmless: a sale committed the instant after an archive lands on a variety that still exists. That is one more history line on an archived variety, a valid state, visible in the books and fixed by restoring. The race that would corrupt data is the delete, and Q1's foreign key closes it. A guarded insert in sales and stock would mean a third author of "archived" inside two other hexagons' SQL for no data-integrity gain. The plan will name this race.
- **Lookups, without a three-state enum.** The callers need one bit more than they have today:
  - **Sales:** `SqlPriceBook.for_variety` answers `None` for an archived variety as for a missing one. `record_sale` raises `UnknownVariety`, so the answer is `sale.variety_not_found` (gone), and Nueva venta's existing override shows the generic line and keeps the form. That is sales.md's rule unchanged.
  - **Stock:** `VarietyCatalog` gains `usable(id)` beside `exists(id)`.
    - Recording an entry needs `usable`.
    - An edit needs `usable` only when `variety_id` changes; keeping its own archived variety needs `exists`.
    - Setting a threshold needs `usable`.
    - Stock's internal signal is re-raised by its router as varieties' `VariedadArchivada` class, the same pattern its `VarietyNotFound` already uses.
  - **Catalog:** `CatalogVarieties.exists` becomes `usable`, so attributes cannot be edited on an archived variety.
  - **Varieties:** recording or deleting a price entry and renaming are refused on an archived variety, through one domain check on `Variety`. Archived varieties are read-only in the domain itself, not only on the page.
- **Codes for `contract.json`:**

  | Code | Status | Kind |
  |---|---|---|
  | `variety.has_history` | 409 | refusal |
  | `variety.name_archived` | 409 | refusal |
  | `variety.archived` | 404 | gone |

  `variety.archived` is answered by varieties, stock and catalog. Sales keeps `sale.variety_not_found`, so Nueva venta's dispatch is untouched.
- **Routes:** `POST /varieties/{id}/archive` (archiving an archived one is idempotent), `POST /varieties/{id}/restore` (likewise), `DELETE /varieties/{id}`, and `GET /varieties?archived=true`.
  - `GET /varieties/{id}` gains `archived: bool` and `removal: "delete" | "archive" | null`. `null` means archived; the value comes from `has_history`, so the API decides the offered action. The list does not carry it, which keeps it free of per-row queries.
  - All of these join the isolation sweep (seeder plus `BODIES`) and the gated-route test.
- **Front path:** `/varieties/archived` goes in `SHARED_FRONT_PATHS` and in `sharedFrontRoutes`. The archived page reuses `^/varieties/[0-9]+$` (ADR-0030), so no new pattern is needed.

**Q5**: Accepted, but I re-cut it. Your (f) disappears, (b) is split by job rather than by hexagon, and archiving is separated from deleting, because the two share only the flag.

| # | Piece | Depends on | Seam |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | Flag and archive/restore in varieties: migration 0012 with its tests; `Variety.archived_at` and the read-only rule; `listed()`, `archived()` and `name_holder`; archive/restore routes and `?archived=true`; codes `variety.archived` and `variety.name_archived`; contract, sweep and gated tests; **ADR-0031** (archive as a stored instant, two named readers with no default, the guarded delete under foreign keys, the accepted archive race) | nothing | domain unit tests; HTTP with `TestClient` over in-memory SQLite and the real adapters |
| B | Delete: `has_history`, `remove_unused`, the `VarietyLeaving` port with its stock and catalog implementations wired in `main.py`, `DELETE /varieties/{id}`, `removal` on the detail, `variety.has_history`, and the foreign-key `IntegrityError` translation in the sales and stock repositories | A (`get`'s state) | the offered-action matrix over HTTP; the race as a repository test over a real SQLite **file** with two connections |
| C | Readers and pickers: `listed()` or `usable()` in catalog, stock rows, `GET /stock?archived=true`, sales' price book and stock's entry and threshold rules; the books-unchanged test; the upkeep trigger matrix for archive and restore through the existing fake Telegram | A | HTTP only, with the before/after capture written first |
| D | Front: the removal action and its two confirmations on the page; Archivadas; the read-only archived page; "Archivadas (N)"; the two-pane selection rules; `screens.json` and `routes.ts`; design comparisons | A, B and C's API | Testing Library with `fetch` faked; the e2e no-curl run |

- B and C run in parallel once A has merged. Each holds three or four production modules plus tests. C crosses three hexagons, but each change there is one or two lines of choosing `listed()` or `usable()`; the weight is the one shared test, which is why it is not split per hexagon.
- **Fakes:** none for `Varieties`. Every seam above is HTTP over the real adapters (§5). The only new fake is the existing Telegram one, reused.
- **Where new code meets old shapes:**
  - Varieties' existing `NameTaken(name)` keeps interpolating the name in `detail` (ADR-0005 allows it); the new refusals carry fixed text.
  - The existing `uq_variety_business_id_name_key` and the `variety` table are unchanged apart from the new column.

**On the proposed invariants**

| Invariant | Decision |
|---|---|
| 1 (no implicit default) | Accepted, enforced by removing `all()` rather than adding a flag. |
| 2 (no filter on period figures, sale views, accounts or the export) | Accepted, proved by the books-unchanged test. |
| 3 (single guarded statement) | Accepted, refined: the guard is in the deciding DELETE, and dependents are deleted earlier in the same transaction. |
| 4 (§11 and a tested downgrade) | Accepted. |

### Round 2: questions

### Round 2: what the answers still break

Failure named: Q2's downgrade is `batch_alter_table("variety").drop_column`. `variety` is the parent of five foreign keys, and ADR-0021 records an open risk of exactly this shape. Turso may not honour `PRAGMA foreign_keys=OFF`, so `DROP TABLE variety` in the rebuild fails on its referencing rows. A downgrade that cannot run in production is not "tested". A second failure is in Q3: the books-unchanged test captures figures that exist today. A figure summed over the stock rows (stock value or capital) would drop silently when `listed()` replaces `all()`, and the test would not see it unless it is in the capture.

❓ **Q1** - **Downgrade over a parent table**: `drop_column` through a batch rebuild drops and recreates `variety` with `foreign_keys=ON` and five referencing tables. ADR-0021 proved the rebuild only locally and flagged Turso as unproven. Modern SQLite (3.35+) has a native `ALTER TABLE ... DROP COLUMN`. What does the downgrade use, and where is it proven (both drivers)?
➡️ Recommendation: emit the native `op.execute("ALTER TABLE variety DROP COLUMN archived_at")` (no rebuild) and test it with both drivers over a seeded business that has rows in all five referencing tables. If libSQL lacks it, state in the ADR that downgrade is restore-only, as ADR-0021 did, and keep the "refuse if any archived" guard.

❓ **Q2** - **Which figures sum over the stock rows?**: Stock rows move to `listed()`. Does anything aggregate over them (a stock total, a capital figure, "lo que te queda" totals, the panel's pending grams) in `assemble.py` or `stock_figures.py`? An archived variety with stock then makes that total fall, which violates criterion 2. Your capture lists the archived variety's stock only through `GET /stock?archived=true`, not through any aggregate.
➡️ Recommendation: grep every consumer of the stock rows list before fixing the design. For each aggregate, either compute it from an unfiltered source (like ADR-0020's scan) or add it to the books-unchanged capture so a drop fails the test. Name each such figure in the plan. Decide which, since "hidden from the list" and "excluded from the total" are different rules.

❓ **Q3** - **Translating the foreign-key IntegrityError**: Q1 turns an `IntegrityError` on `sale_line`/`stock_entry` insert into the gone code. A foreign-key failure there can also come from `customer_id`, `business_id` or another FK, and SQLite's message does not name the column. How does the repository know it was the variety? Does a deleted-then-rereferenced price entry, or a sale edit, hit the same path?
➡️ Recommendation: do not infer from the exception. After catching the `IntegrityError`, outside the `except` block, re-check with the existing lookup (`for_variety` / `usable`). If the variety is gone, raise the gone code. Otherwise re-raise, so a customer FK bug stays a 500. Test both branches, plus the two-connection race in both orders.

❓ **Q4** - **Rollback after partial deletes, on both drivers**: The use case deletes thresholds, attributes and price entries, then `remove_unused` finds history and the use case rolls back. Two unknowns: (a) the Session's ORM state for `price_entry` rows already deleted and flushed (the `save` path rebuilds `row.price_entries`), and (b) `DELETE ... RETURNING` support and rollback semantics on the Turso driver. Is a refusal for a variety with history the common path? It is the case where all earlier deletes are wasted and rolled back.
➡️ Recommendation: run the guard first as a read-only `has_history` inside the same transaction as the writes. Take the write lock with a no-op write, or accept the check-then-delete order, and keep `remove_unused` as the final guard. The fast refusal then rolls back nothing. Prove `RETURNING` and rollback on the libSQL driver in a test, not in prose.

❓ **Q5** - **Piece A is too big**: A holds the migration, `tables.py`, `variety.py` (flag and read-only rule), `ports.py`, `manage_varieties.py`/`manage_prices.py`, `sql_varieties.py` (`listed`, `archived`, `name_holder`), `errors.py`, `schemas.py`, `router.py`, `contract.json`, sweep tests and ADR-0031. That is about nine production modules plus tests plus an ADR, well over the cap, and it blocks B, C and D.
➡️ Recommendation: split A in two. A1 is the flag and its plumbing: migration, `tables.py`, `Variety.archived_at`, `listed()`/`archived()` replacing `all()` with its four callers moved, and `name_holder`. A2 is the archive/restore behaviour: use cases, routes, codes, contract and ADR-0031. The seam between them is the port. Which of the two carries the ADR, and does B depend on A1 only?

### Invariants proposed
- A migration that drops or adds a column on a table referenced by foreign keys is tested upgrade and downgrade over rows in every referencing table, on both drivers.
- A translated `IntegrityError` becomes a domain refusal only after a re-read confirms the cause, and any other cause is re-raised.

### Round 2: answers

**Q1**: Accepted, with one change: I prove it on both local drivers and call the remote one unproven.
- **Downgrade:** `op.execute("ALTER TABLE variety DROP COLUMN archived_at")`, the native drop with no rebuild.
  - `archived_at` carries no index, constraint or foreign key, which is the only case where SQLite (3.35+) allows that statement.
  - With no rebuild, `DROP TABLE variety` never runs, so ADR-0021's risk about `foreign_keys=OFF` on Turso does not arise.
  - The upgrade is the matching native `op.add_column`, also with no rebuild.
  - The guard stays: the downgrade refuses, with a fixed message, if any `archived_at IS NOT NULL`.
- **Proof:** `tests/migrations/test_0012_archived_variety.py`, parameterised over a `sqlite:///` file and a `sqlite+libsql:///` file.
  - It upgrades to 0011 and seeds two businesses with a row in each of the five tables that reference `variety`: `price_entry`, `sale_line`, `stock_entry`, `stock_threshold` and `catalog_attributes`.
  - It upgrades to head, archives one variety and asserts that the downgrade refuses. It then restores, downgrades, and asserts row counts and `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` are clean. It upgrades again.
  - Both drivers run on every test run.
- ADR-0031 records that Turso's *remote* protocol is not exercised by tests, the same honesty ADR-0021 had. With no rebuild, the only failure left would be a libSQL server too old for `DROP COLUMN`.

**Q2**: I checked every consumer of the stock rows. Exactly one aggregate sums over them: `read_stock`'s `total`, answered by `GET /stock` (stock.md: "the sum of every variety's stock. The API answers it; no screen shows it").
- **Rule:** an archived variety is hidden from the list but **not** left out of the total. "Every variety's stock" plus criterion 2 ("stock figures… read exactly as before") settles it, and archiving says the stock "queda guardado".
- **How:** `total` is summed over every variety in `grouped`, the `lots_and_draws` of `for_varieties(None)`. That is an unfiltered source, as ADR-0020's scan is. It is no longer summed over the listed rows, so the loop that builds the rows and the loop that builds the total stop sharing a filter. A variety with no movement adds 0 either way.
- **Proof:** `GET /stock`'s `total` joins the books-unchanged capture.
- **The other consumers have no aggregate:**

  | Consumer | Why nothing can drop |
  |---|---|
  | `read_panel` | passes `stock_answer.rows` through `sorted_by_name` and sums nothing (results.md: "There is no total grams") |
  | Panel's pending grams and cost notes | come from the scan over `for_varieties(None)` draws, which never read the rows |
  | Catalog | counts nothing |
  | Variedades' summary line | counts unarchived varieties only, by the PRD's own words, so it is meant to change |

- **The plan's list of figures that must not move** holds:
  - the stock `total`;
  - every `GET /analysis` row;
  - `GET /panel`'s period figures;
  - each sale's lines and costs;
  - each customer's balance and account;
  - the archived variety's own row through `GET /stock?archived=true`.

**Q3**: Accepted, with one detail you missed: the session after a failed flush. Once a flush raises, the SQLAlchemy session is unusable until rolled back, so "re-check with the existing lookup" cannot run inside the repository.
- **Repository:** the sales and stock repositories catch `IntegrityError` around the flush only to set a flag. Outside the `except`, they raise an internal, code-less `ReferenceRefused` signal. This is the same shape as `sql_customers._flush_or_mark_taken` (ADR-0005: nothing of the driver's message stays reachable).
- **Use case:** `record_sale`, `record_entry` and `edit_entry` catch `ReferenceRefused`, call `transaction.rollback()` (the port already has it), and re-ask their own lookup (`price_book.for_variety` / `catalog.usable`).
  - If the variety is gone or archived, they raise the gone code: `sale.variety_not_found` or `variety.not_found`.
  - Otherwise they re-raise the original. A customer or other foreign-key bug stays a 500 and is never dressed as "gone".
- **Price entries** are written through the `Variety` aggregate in varieties' own repository. A price entry recorded against a variety deleted meanwhile takes the same path in `record_price`, with `get` as the lookup.
- **Sales:** a sale is never edited, so the only sales path is recording a sale. Assigning a customer touches no variety.
- **Tests:**
  - both branches as use-case tests, each with a fake whose flush raises the signal: the lookup says gone → gone code; the lookup says present → re-raised;
  - the two-connection race over a real SQLite file in both orders (delete first → the insert is refused as gone; insert first → the delete is refused with `variety.has_history`), on both drivers.

**Q4**: Accepted.
- **Order inside `delete_variety`:**
  1. `get`: missing → `variety.not_found`; archived → `variety.archived`.
  2. `has_history`, a read. History → `variety.has_history` with nothing written, so the common refusal rolls back nothing.
  3. The dependents, through `VarietyLeaving`, then varieties' own price entries.
  4. `remove_unused` as the final guard.

  If step 4 returns no row, the use case rolls back and refuses with the same code. That only happens on the race. I will not add a no-op write to take the lock: the final guard already makes the outcome correct, and taking the lock early only moves the window.
- **ORM state:** every delete in this path is a Core `delete()` statement, never a mutation of `row.price_entries`, so the `delete-orphan` cascade and the identity map are not involved. The use case reads no ORM object after its first write. Commit and rollback both expire the session, and the request ends there. `save` is never called on this path.
- **Proof, both drivers:** a repository test over a `sqlite+libsql:///` file and a `sqlite:///` file asserts two things:
  - `remove_unused` returns the id with no history, and nothing when there is history;
  - after a forced rollback following steps 3 and 4, the thresholds, attributes and price entries are all still there.

  `RETURNING` on libSQL has precedent: `SqlPayments` and business removal rely on it, and ADR-0016's note says so. It is still tested here, not argued.

**Q5**: Accepted, with one move: the `variety.archived` code goes into A1, so A2, B and C all depend on A1 only and run in parallel.
- **A1, the flag and its readers.** It carries **ADR-0031**.
  - It holds the migration with its tests, `tables.py`, `Variety.archived_at` and `is_archived`, and `listed()`/`archived()`/`name_holder` replacing `all()`/`name_taken` in the port and `SqlVarieties`.
  - The `VarietyArchived` class (`variety.archived`, 404, gone) goes in `errors.py`, with its `TranslateErrors` registration and its `contract.json` line.
  - The callers move, each forced by `mypy --strict`: catalog's `on`, stock's `ids` (now `listed_ids`), `SqlStockFigures` and `read_stock`, whose `total` changes as in Q2.
  - Substantive modules: the migration, `variety.py`, `ports.py` and `sql_varieties.py`. The rest are one-line call-site changes.
  - ADR-0031 lands here because the column and the no-default readers are what is hard to change. It also records, as decisions B and C follow:
    - the guarded delete under foreign keys;
    - the use case's re-check after a refused reference;
    - the accepted archive race;
    - the stock-total rule.
- **A2, archiving and restoring.**
  - The domain's read-only rule on an archived `Variety`: rename and price add or delete are refused.
  - The archive and restore use cases and routes, and `?archived=true`.
  - `variety.name_archived`.
  - The isolation sweep and the gated-route test.
- **B, deleting.** As in round 1, plus Q3 and Q4. It depends on A1 only, since it needs `get`'s state and `VarietyArchived`.
- **C, the readers and pickers.** It holds `usable()` in sales, stock and catalog, `GET /stock?archived=true`, the books-unchanged test and the upkeep matrix. It depends on A1 only.
- **D, the front.** Last.

**Invariants**
- The first invariant (migrations over parent tables) is accepted, applied to this migration and kept in ADR-0031.
- The second (re-read before translating an `IntegrityError`) is accepted, with the re-read in the use case after a rollback, not in the repository.

### Round 3: questions

### Round 3: the split's dependencies and one gap in the state machine

Failure named: the split says C (readers, pickers, the books-unchanged test) depends on A1 only. But after A1 nothing can archive a variety through HTTP, because the archive route is in A2. C's capstone test, which archives, captures and restores through the API, cannot run on C's branch. Either C quietly merges after A2, or the test is written against a hand-seeded `archived_at` and never exercises the real route. Criterion 2 is proved only by that test, so this is the weakest joint of the plan.

❓ **Q1** - **Where the books-unchanged test lives**: It needs the archive and restore routes (A2) and every hiding reader (A1, C). Your table says C depends on A1 only, and C owns the test.
➡️ Recommendation: C writes the per-reader hiding tests, seeding `archived_at` through the table directly. The capstone, the before/after/after-restore capture through the real routes, is a separate last piece (or the head of D) that depends on A2 and C. It is the final review gate for criterion 2. State which branch it lives on and who reviews it, because a reviewer sees one branch only.

❓ **Q2** - **Parallel branches that edit the same files**: A1, A2 and B all touch `app/varieties/application/ports.py`, `errors.py`, `manage_varieties.py`, `adapters/router.py`, `schemas.py`, `sql_varieties.py` and `web/src/api/contract.json`. A1 and A2 both register codes, and B adds `has_history`/`remove_unused` to the same port that A1 reshapes (`all()` → `listed()`/`archived()`). Parallel after A1 means two branches rewrite the same lines, and the conflicts resolve at merge, where no reviewer is looking.
➡️ Recommendation: serialise what shares a file. Run A1, then A2 and B one after the other (or have B rebase on A2), and keep only C genuinely parallel, since it touches other hexagons. Alternatively have A1 land the `Varieties` port methods B needs as stubs. Say which, and say which codes each piece adds to `contract.json` so no code is registered twice.

❓ **Q3** - **Archive on a variety with no history, and the delete/archive matrix**: The PRD offers delete when there is no history and archive when there is. You decided `POST /varieties/{id}/archive` is idempotent on an archived variety. Nothing says what the API does when the variety has no history, or when an archived variety's history disappears (a stock entry deleted, if entries can be deleted). Delete on an archived variety answers 404 `variety.archived`, yet an archived one with no history has no offered action at all (`removal: null`).
➡️ Recommendation: write the full matrix (live/archived × history/none × archive/restore/delete) in the plan, one expected answer per cell, and test it as a table. For cells the PRD does not cover (archive with no history through the API), consult PM instead of choosing. My reading: refuse with a fixed-text refusal, because the page never offers it and a hand-made request should not leave a history-less archived variety that cannot be deleted.

❓ **Q4** - **Piece D**: The front holds the removal action with two confirmations, Archivadas, the read-only archived page, "Archivadas (N)", the two-pane selection rules, `screens.json`, `routes.ts`, design comparisons, and the e2e no-curl run. That is far more than four production modules (several screens and the shared `api` layer) plus tests.
➡️ Recommendation: split D by screen. D1 is the removal action on the variety page plus the `removal` and `archived` fields in the API types. D2 is the Archivadas list, the "Archivadas (N)" entry and the read-only page, with the `routes.ts` and `screens.json` entries. The e2e run joins the capstone from Q1. Which of D1 and D2 owns the selection rules, and does either depend on the other?

### Invariants proposed
(none this round)

### Round 3: answers

**Q1**: Accepted. The before/after capture becomes its own last backend piece, **E**.
- **C keeps the per-reader hiding tests.** It seeds `archived_at` through the table, using a small test helper under `tests/fakes/`, never inside a production module, and asserts each reader:
  - `GET /varieties`;
  - `GET /catalog` (the text and the "does not go out" list);
  - the panel's stock rows;
  - `GET /stock` rows, and its `total` unchanged;
  - `GET /stock?archived=true`;
  - the sales price book and stock's `usable` for new records.
- **E, "the books don't move"**, depends on A2 and C both merged. It branches from `master` after them, so its reviewer sees every reader and the real routes in one tree.
  - It holds the capture of round 2's Q2 list through `POST /varieties/{id}/archive` and `/restore`, before, after archiving and after restoring, with 0 differences.
  - It also holds the upkeep check: the pin is edited on archive and on restore exactly when the text changes, through the existing fake Telegram.
  - It adds no production code. If it finds a figure that moves, the fix goes in E with the test, and the reviewer judges both together.
  - E's reviewer is the gate for criterion 2.
- **The e2e no-curl run** belongs to D2 (Q4), the last front piece, since it needs the screens.

**Q2**: Accepted; I serialise the varieties files. Order: **A1 → A2 → B**, each branching from `master` after the previous one merged. No stubs: a stub port method lands an untested promise and still conflicts when it is filled in.
- **C runs in parallel with A2 and B after A1.** It touches only stock's and catalog's adapters and routers and sales' price book, never a file under `app/varieties/`.
  - Stock's router re-raises A1's `VarietyArchived` the way it already re-raises `VarietyNotFound`.
  - Stock's schema gains `?archived=true`.
- **E** follows A2 and C. **D1** follows B and E. **D2** follows D1.
- **`contract.json` lines, each added exactly once:**

  | Code | Piece | Status | Kind |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `variety.archived` | A1 | 404 | gone |
  | `variety.name_archived` | A2 | 409 | refusal |
  | `variety.no_history` | A2 (archive refused for a variety with no history; see Q3) | 409 | refusal |
  | `variety.has_history` | B | 409 | refusal |

  C, E and D add none. C reuses `variety.not_found`, `variety.archived` and `sale.variety_not_found`.
  - `tests/test_refusal_codes.py` already fails if a code is registered twice or is missing from the JSON.

**Q3**: Accepted, with one difference from your reading. Most cells are settled by the PRD; only the stale-page archive is open, and it goes to PM.
- **The PRD settles these:**
  - archive is "for a variety with history", so an archive request for a live variety with no history is refused by the domain;
  - an archived variety can lose all its history, since its entries stay editable and deletable from Todas las entradas and its sales stay deletable;
  - deleting it then is "restore first, then delete; there is no second path".

  So `removal: null` on an archived variety is correct whatever its history: its one action is Restaurar.
- **Restore and archive are idempotent.** A partner may have done the same thing a second earlier; the end state is the one asked for, and the toast stays true.
- **The matrix**, tested as one parameterised table at the HTTP seam in A2 for archive and restore and in B for delete:

  | State | `POST …/archive` | `POST …/restore` | `DELETE /varieties/{id}` | `removal` on GET |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | live, with history | 200, archived | 200, no change | 409 `variety.has_history` | `archive` |
  | live, no history | 409 `variety.no_history` (provisional, PM) | 200, no change | 204, gone for good | `delete` |
  | archived, with history | 200, no change | 200, restored as it was | 404 `variety.archived` | `null` |
  | archived, no history | 200, no change | 200, restored as it was | 404 `variety.archived` | `null` |
  | missing, or another business's | 404 `variety.not_found` | 404 `variety.not_found` | 404 `variety.not_found` | 404 |

- **The open cell, sent to PM.** A page loaded with history shows "Archivar variedad"; a partner then deletes the variety's last sale or entry, and the seller taps Archivar. The PRD defines the mirror case for delete (refuse, refresh, show "Archivar variedad") but not this one.
  - Until PM answers, I plan the mirror: refuse with `variety.no_history`, refresh the page, show "Eliminar variedad". That needs no new screen state.
  - If PM prefers to archive anyway, that cell becomes "200, archived" and A2 drops `variety.no_history`. Nothing else in the matrix moves.

**Q4**: Accepted, with the dependency stated: D2 follows D1.
- **D1, the removal action on a live variety's page:**
  - the `archived` and `removal` fields in the API types;
  - "Eliminar variedad" with its destructive ConfirmDialog and "Archivar variedad" with its confirmation that names the grams;
  - the toasts;
  - the stale-page refusals (`variety.has_history` → refresh; Q3's cell as PM decides);
  - the gone dispatch;
  - the computer's selection after archiving or deleting (next by name, else previous, else the empty state).

  Variedades' first selection on a computer needs no change, because `GET /varieties` now answers only unarchived varieties.
- **D2, Archivadas and the archived page:**
  - "Archivadas (N)" at the foot of Variedades, from `GET /varieties?archived=true`;
  - the Archivadas card with Restaurar, and its own first-archived selection in two panes;
  - the read-only archived page: it reuses `VariedadDetalle` with every action hidden, shows "Archivada" in words, has "‹ Archivadas", and a back with no history goes to Archivadas;
  - `routes.ts` and `screens.json`;
  - the backend's `SHARED_FRONT_PATHS` entry `/varieties/archived` and `sharedFrontRoutes`. They land with the front route, as ADR-0030 requires;
  - the design comparisons for all four screens, phone and computer, both themes;
  - the e2e no-curl run.
- **Why D2 follows D1:** the read-only page has to hide the removal action that D1 adds.
- **Prerequisite:** the `pantallas/` files for the Archivadas card and the archived page are design's work and must exist before D2 starts. The plan names them as D2's entry condition.
- Each piece is about four production front modules plus tests: D1 is the page, the confirmation copy, the API types and the two-pane selection; D2 is the card, the archived page mode, the routes and the list's foot entry.

### Round 3: PM answers

### Answers to the Tech Lead (round 3)

**Q1: archiving a variety whose history disappeared after the page loaded.** The answer is **(b)**: the app archives it anyway.

- **Why.** The seller tapped Archivar to get the variety out of their lists, and archiving does that whether or not the variety still has history.
  - Archiving loses nothing, and it can always be undone with Restaurar.
  - Refusing it would add a refusal and a refresh that protect nothing.
  - An archived variety with no history can already happen. A seller can delete an archived variety's last stock entry from Todas las entradas, or its last sale. The interview settled that case: restore first, then delete. So (b) adds no new state.
- **The rule, made precise.**
  - History decides which removal action the page *offers*. It is worked out when the page is read.
  - Only **delete** is guarded by the domain.
  - **Archive** is never refused because of history, whether there is some or none.
  - "The app never offers a delete that will be refused" still holds. The only refusal a seller can meet is the stale-page delete, which already refreshes to show "Archivar variedad".
- **Nothing else changes.**
  - A variety already gone still answers "Eso ya no está".
  - Archiving a variety that is already archived is a no-op.
  - Restoring brings it back. Its page then offers "Eliminar variedad", because it has no history.

`varieties.md` is updated to say this. The glossary's **Archived variety** no longer says it always has history.

### Closing

### Closing summary (round 4, last round)

**A failure that stands until the plan is amended.** Your matrix still has the provisional cell "live, no history → `POST …/archive` 409 `variety.no_history`". PM's answer to round 3 Q1 overrules it: archive is never refused because of history. If A2 ships that cell, it breaks the PRD and registers a dead code in `contract.json`. The amendments below are part of the decision.

**Decided**
- **Flag and migration (A1).**
  - `variety.archived_at` is a nullable `UtcDateTime`, added with a native `ADD COLUMN`.
  - The downgrade is a native `DROP COLUMN` that refuses while any variety is archived.
  - It is proved on the `sqlite` and `libsql` file drivers with rows in all five referencing tables.
  - `uq_variety_business_id_name_key` is untouched, so an archived variety keeps its name. `name_holder` distinguishes live from archived holders.
- **No implicit filter.** `all()` is removed. `listed()` and `archived()` replace it, which makes every caller choose under `mypy --strict`.
  - `listed()` is used by `GET /varieties`, the catalog, the panel's stock rows and `GET /stock` rows.
  - Readers that only join for a name (period figures, sale views, accounts, export) are not filtered.
  - The stock `total` is summed from the unfiltered `for_varieties(None)` source, so archiving does not move it.
- **Archive and restore (A2).**
  - Both are idempotent.
  - Archived varieties are read-only in the domain: rename and price changes are refused.
  - `variety.name_archived` is registered.
  - Per PM: **archive is never refused because of history**. The cell "live, no history → archive" becomes `200, archived`, and `variety.no_history` is dropped from A2 and `contract.json`. The page offers the action from `removal`, and only delete is guarded by the domain.
- **Delete (B).**
  - `has_history` is read first, so the common refusal writes nothing.
  - Dependents go through `VarietyLeaving`, then the price entries, then a guarded `DELETE … NOT (history) RETURNING`.
  - `variety.has_history` is registered.
  - The foreign key closes the race with a concurrent sale or entry. The use case rolls back, re-asks its own lookup, and re-raises when the cause is not the variety.
- **Stale references (C).**
  - `usable()` is added in sales, stock and catalog, with `variety.archived` as the gone answer.
  - Archiving an in-flight record is a harmless, accepted race.
- **Split and order:** A1 → A2 → B (they share files and are serialised). C runs in parallel after A1. E ("the books don't move", no production code) follows A2 and C. D1 follows B and E, and D2 follows D1.
- **ADR-0031** lands in A1, in the Tech Lead's words, covering:
  - the stored instant;
  - the named readers with no default;
  - the guarded delete under foreign keys;
  - the accepted archive race;
  - the stock-total rule;
  - Turso's remote protocol as untested.

**Known risks**
- Turso's remote protocol is never exercised by tests, and a libSQL server too old for `DROP COLUMN` would make the downgrade restore-only.
- A sale committed the instant after an archive lands on an archived variety. This is valid and accepted, but it is a visible history line on an archived variety.
- D2 cannot start until design delivers the Archivadas card and archived-page files.
- E is the only proof of criterion 2. A figure outside its capture list can still move unnoticed, so the list in round 2 Q2 must be complete.

### Invariants proposed
- The archive use case and route never refuse because the variety has or lacks history; only the delete is guarded by history.

## Tech Lead

### Approach

Archiving is one stored fact: a nullable instant, `variety.archived_at`. Whether a variety has history is never stored; it is worked out live from `sale_line` and `stock_entry`. Readers choose between two named methods, `listed()` and `archived()`. `Varieties.all()` is removed, so under `mypy --strict` every caller has to pick one and nothing filters by default.

Archive and restore are idempotent and never refused because of history (PM, round 3). History decides only which removal action the page *offers*. Only delete is guarded:
- it reads `has_history` first, so the common refusal writes nothing;
- it deletes each owner's dependents;
- it ends with one guarded `DELETE … AND NOT (history) RETURNING id`.

The foreign keys (`PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON`) close the race between that delete and a concurrent sale or entry.

### What moves and what never moves

| Reader | Archived varieties |
|---|---|
| `GET /varieties` (list, Nueva venta, stock entry and price pickers, "Arrancá por las variedades") | hidden (`listed()`) |
| `GET /varieties?archived=true` | only archived (`archived()`) |
| Catalog text, its "does not go out" list, the pinned catalog | hidden |
| Panel's stock rows (Lo que te queda), `GET /stock` rows | hidden |
| `GET /stock?archived=true` | archived rows only |
| `GET /stock`'s `total` | **counted**, summed from the unfiltered `for_varieties(None)` load |
| Period figures (ADR-0020 scan), `GET /analysis`, `GET /panel` periods, sale views, accounts, Todas las entradas, the export | untouched, no filter |

### Removal matrix (tested as one parameterised table)

| State | `POST …/archive` | `POST …/restore` | `DELETE /varieties/{id}` | `removal` on `GET /varieties/{id}` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| live, with history | 200, archived | 200, no change | 409 `variety.has_history` | `"archive"` |
| live, no history | 200, archived | 200, no change | 204, deleted | `"delete"` |
| archived, with history | 200, no change | 200, restored | 404 `variety.archived` | `null` |
| archived, no history | 200, no change | 200, restored | 404 `variety.archived` | `null` |
| missing or another business's | 404 `variety.not_found` | 404 `variety.not_found` | 404 `variety.not_found` | 404 |

### Refusal codes (each added once, in the piece named)

| Code | Status | Kind | Piece |
|---|---|---|---|
| `variety.archived` | 404 | gone | A1 |
| `variety.name_archived` | 409 | refusal | A2 |
| `variety.has_history` | 409 | refusal | B |

There is no `variety.no_history`. PM ruled that archive is never refused.

### Order

**A1 → A2 → B.** All three share the varieties files, so they run one after another.

**C** starts after A1 and runs alongside A2. B also waits for C, because both touch stock's `manage_entries`.

**E** depends on A2 and C: the "books don't move" capture through the real routes, with no production code. **D1** depends on B and E. **D2** depends on D1.

### Where new code meets old shapes

- Varieties' existing `NameTaken(name)` keeps interpolating the name in `detail` (ADR-0005 allows it). The new refusals carry fixed text.
- `uq_variety_business_id_name_key` and the existing `variety` columns stay as they are.
- Stock's code-less internal signals keep their pattern: the router translates them into varieties' classes.

### Security and performance (§8)

- **Isolation.** The new routes widen nothing across businesses. Each takes `CurrentBusinessId`, and every statement filters on it.
- **Front path.** `/varieties/archived` becomes an exact shared front path (ADR-0030). It serves the static shell to browser navigations only.
- **Delete cost.** One delete costs two `EXISTS` probes plus a constant number of statements.
- **Total cost.** The stock `total` keeps its O(entries + lines) shape.
- **Logging.** Nothing is logged; observability is unchanged.

### Known risks

- **Turso's remote protocol is never exercised by tests.** A libSQL server too old for `DROP COLUMN` would make the downgrade restore-only.
- **A sale or entry can land on an archived variety.** If one is committed the instant after an archive, it adds a history line to the archived variety. This is accepted and visible.
- **E's capture list is the only proof of criterion 2.** A figure outside that list could move unnoticed.
- **D2 is blocked on design.** It cannot start until `pantallas/` holds the Archivadas card and the archived page.

### Subtasks

`### Subtasks` written by the system from the structured plan: no exact `### Subtasks` line (outside code fences) was found in this section, so the block below was built from the structured plan. Any list the Tech Lead wrote under another heading is not the one the Developers read.

#### The archived flag, its migration, named readers replacing all(), and ADR-0031

Add the stored fact and make every reader of varieties choose explicitly. No route can archive yet; that is A2.

**Migration `0012`** (revises 0011), `app/migrations/versions/`:
- **Upgrade:** a native `op.add_column('variety', archived_at UtcDateTime nullable)`. No server default, no backfill, no batch rebuild.
- **Downgrade:** first check `SELECT 1 FROM variety WHERE archived_at IS NOT NULL`. If any row matches, raise with a fixed message naming no data. Otherwise `op.execute("ALTER TABLE variety DROP COLUMN archived_at")` (native, no rebuild).
- **Test:** `tests/migrations/test_0012_archived_variety.py`, parameterised over a `sqlite:///` file and a `sqlite+libsql:///` file.
  1. Upgrade to 0011, then seed two businesses with a row in each of `price_entry`, `sale_line`, `stock_entry`, `stock_threshold` and `catalog_attributes`.
  2. Upgrade to head (through the real `upgrade_to_head`). Set one `archived_at` and assert the downgrade refuses.
  3. Clear it and downgrade. Assert row counts are unchanged and `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` is empty.
  4. Upgrade again.
- The naming test (§11) must pass with no allowlist change.

**Table and domain:**
- `VarietyRow.archived_at` (`UtcDateTime | None`).
- `Variety` gains `archived_at: datetime | None` and an `is_archived` property.

**Port (`app/varieties/application/ports.py`) and `SqlVarieties`:**
- Remove `all()`.
- Add `listed()` (unarchived, by `name_key`) and `archived()` (archived, by `name_key`).
- `get(id)` answers any variety of the business, archived or not.
- Replace `name_taken` with `name_holder(name, *, other_than=None) -> NameHolder | None`, where `NameHolder` is an enum `LIVE | ARCHIVED` in the domain or application.
- `register_variety` and `rename_variety` raise `NameTaken` for any holder. A2 splits `ARCHIVED` into its own code.

**Error:** `VarietyArchived(LookupError)`, code `variety.archived`, in `app/varieties/application/errors.py`.
- Register it in the varieties router's `TranslateErrors` under 404.
- Add it to `web/src/api/contract.json` with kind `gone`, and add its entry to `web/src/api/refusals.ts` (the generic gone dispatch), so `tsc` passes.

**Move every caller of `all()`:**
- `list_varieties` → `listed()`. `GET /varieties` keeps its shape.
- `SqlCatalogVarieties.on` → `listed()`.
- `SqlStockFigures.read`'s rows → `listed()`.
- `SqlVarietyCatalog.ids` becomes `listed_ids()` over `listed()`.
- `read_stock`'s `total` is summed over every variety key in `grouped` (the unfiltered `for_varieties(None)` load), no longer over the listed rows. Rows and total stop sharing a filter.
- Confirm that `app/varieties/adapters/import_port.py`'s `has_records` counts the whole `variety` table, archived included. Make it an explicit count if it went through `all()`.

**Business removal (ADR-0015):** add `variety.archived_at` to the migrated-schema classification tests' expectations. It is exported as an ISO instant with no secret flag, which proves the export picks it up with no change to the command.

**ADR-0031**, `docs/adr/0031-*.md`, records:
- archiving as a stored nullable instant;
- history computed on read;
- `listed()`/`archived()` with no default and `all()` removed;
- the stock total summed from the unfiltered load;
- the native ADD and DROP COLUMN, with the downgrade refusing while any variety is archived;
- the delete guarded in one `DELETE … NOT (history) RETURNING` after dependents, with the foreign key closing the insert race;
- the use case's rollback-and-reask after a refused reference;
- the accepted archive race (a sale landing just after an archive);
- archive never refused for history (PM);
- Turso's remote protocol as untested.

**Acceptance:**
- The existing suite passes unchanged in behaviour.
- With `archived_at` set by hand in a test, the variety is absent from `GET /varieties` and still counted in `GET /stock`'s `total`.
- `mypy --strict` and `ruff` are clean.

Seams: `Alembic upgrade/downgrade through `upgrade_to_head` over sqlite and libsql files`, ``Variety` domain unit tests`, `HTTP `GET /varieties` and `GET /stock` with TestClient`.

Depends on: nothing.
